# Preface
## To the Reader.
### Honored Reader.

I hereby present this little treatise by an anonymous philosopher, <term>Philalethes<note>original: "Philalethæ" (Latin genitive). A famous pseudonym meaning "lover of truth," used by several alchemical authors, most notably Eirenaeus Philalethes.</note></term>, in the German language, which I first released to the world in Latin some time ago. Having been sufficiently informed of how it has been greatly praised, well-received, and highly esteemed by many—especially by scholars and those particularly devoted to the <term>alchemical science<note>original: "Alchymistischen Wissenschafft"</note></term>—I have therefore found cause and deemed it good to translate it into our dear mother tongue for the benefit and use of our fellow Germans. My hope is that perhaps some faithful hearts and industrious workers may be strengthened in their good intentions, while others who have gone astray may finally be converted from their imagined, presumptuous thoughts and unfounded <term>messy operations<note>original: "Sudel-Wercken." This term was often used by "philosophical" alchemists to mock "puffers"—those who mindlessly experimented with chemicals without understanding the underlying theory.</note></term>. To be sure, a few may have criticized and rejected it; yet certainly this arises from a perverse judgment, as those who, out of excessive blindness and false—

<summary>This preface introduces a German translation of an alchemical treatise by the pseudonymous author Philalethes, intended to guide serious practitioners and discourage fraudulent "messy" alchemy.</summary>
<keywords>Philalethes, alchemy, German translation, laboratory practice, preface, 17th-century science</keywords>

out of imaginary *experience* <note>original: "Experienz"</note> destroy that which, in their ignorance, they could and should use to correct their own errors; since it cannot be denied that our *Philaletha* <note>The author Eirenaeus Philalethes.</note> speaks from certain experience after his completed work and the successfully harvested *gem* <note>original: "Kleinod." In alchemical literature, this "gem" or "treasure" refers to the Philosopher’s Stone or the successful completion of the Great Work.</note>, while others may watch how they arrange their course within self-chosen boundaries, so that they do not perhaps stumble at the very goal and spill their courage, hope, and property.

Moreover, and so that no one might think that *Philaletha* is not so much to be relied upon because he occasionally uses certain sayings and modes of expression that are otherwise not found among most ancient *Alchemists*, I have—to prove that he is in agreement with the ancients in all things, or at least in each and every main point—added to this another little treatise, which I also present for the first time in the German language: namely, that of a monk, Brother *Ferrarius* <note>Likely Efferarius Monachus, a medieval alchemist whose works were highly regarded in the 17th century.</note>. He is so named not only by *Combachius* <note>Johann Combach (1585–1651), a German philosopher and editor of alchemical texts.</note>, who last brought him more perfectly to the light from a written *copy* <note>original: "Exemplar"</note> by *Nicolaus May*, formerly a counselor to *Emperor Rudolph* the Second of most glorious memory <note>Rudolph II (1552–1612) was a famous patron of alchemy, occultism, and the arts in Prague.</note>; but he is also cited under this name in the *Illustrated Rosary* <note>original: "Rosario figurato," likely referring to the *Rosarium philosophorum*, a famous series of alchemical woodcuts.</note> and by *Johannes Baptista Nazarius* <note>Giovanni Battista Nazari, author of "Della Tramutatione Metallica" (1572).</note> according to the opinion of *P. Borellus* <note>Pierre Borel (c. 1620–1671), a French scientist and bibliographer of alchemy.</note>. Elsewhere, in the printed *copies* <note>original: "Exemplarien"</note> at *Basel* and *Strasbourg*, and also in many new writings and citations, he has until—

...her <note>The text begins with the suffix "-her," likely completing a word such as "further" or "afterward" from the preceding page.</note> named **Efferarius** and **Euferarius**; it deals with the **Philosopher’s Stone**, and how one should prepare it in the proper manner. In his opinions, he particularly follows—according to his own confession—**Gebro Abenhaen** <note>original: "Gebro Abenhaen." This refers to Jabir ibn Hayyan, the 8th-century polymath known in the West as Geber, the "Father of Chemistry."</note>, a world-renowned and so well-proven Alchemist that he is not only called a "Master of all Masters" by the most prominent **Philosophers** <note>In this context, "Philosophers" refers to "Natural Philosophers" or alchemists who studied the secrets of nature.</note> even today, but was also held to be a king in his art during his own time. For it is presumably to be understood in this way when he is called "King **Geber**" by the **Saracen**, **Arabian**, and oldest **Latin Chemists** <note>original: "Chymicis."</note>. Otherwise, it would not be easy to believe that he—who was a Greek by birth and had renounced his faith—should have immediately received a kingdom among the **Saracens** <note>A medieval term for Arab Muslims.</note> after adopting their faith, especially since he is said to have lived a hundred years after their **Muhammad** <note>original: "Mahumet."</note>, as reported by **Joannes Leo** <note>Leo Africanus (c. 1494–1554), a famous traveler and scholar whose descriptions of Africa and Islam were highly influential in Europe.</note>, a native **African** and converted **Christian**. At that time, they suffered losses everywhere and were pushed quite into a corner. 

Our **Ferrarius** has followed this man in his opinions and well-founded labor, and without doubt obtained his desire according to his wish and will; he also composed his writings for the benefit first of his Pope <note>Likely referring to Pope John XXII, who, despite his official decrees against alchemical counterfeiters, was legendary for having allegedly amassed a fortune through the "Art."</note> and then for posterity, both of which are in this little treatise; which, truly, is full of beautiful Philosophical sayings, pleasant certain principles, and

# Preface to the Reader.

...of quiet manual operations <note>original: "stiller Handgriffe." This refers to the subtle laboratory techniques required in alchemy.</note>; as well as in the *Treasure of the Wise* <note>original: "Schatz der Weisen."</note> or of Wisdom / which is found in the *Chemical Theater* <note>original: "Theatro Chymico," a famous multi-volume collection of alchemical tracts.</note>, and in the second volume of the book titled *Of Alchemy, as they call it, and the Doctrine of the Metallic Art* <note>original: "Alchimiæ, quam vocant, artisque metallicæ doctrinæ." The OCR "vacant" is likely a typo for "vocant."</note> / and printed in Basel by Peter Perna in the year 1572 in octavo [8vo] in Latin; however, in the third part of the *Golden Ark* by Claude Dariot <note>original: "güldenen Arche Claudii Darioti," referring to a collection of alchemical works edited by Dariot, a French physician.</note>, and in the book called the *Golden Fleece* <note>original: "Aureum Vellus"</note> / [it] is to be found in German / though translated in a completely incomprehensible and shapeless manner. 

Otherwise, I have chosen to call this entire book the *Chemical Two-Leaf* <note>original: "Chymisches Zwey-Blat." A "Zwey-Blat" or "double leaf" suggests a pairing of two complementary texts.</note> / because it contains two chemical or alchemical writers—one New and one Old / who nevertheless harmonize quite skillfully in their opinions / though not in their words / as mentioned above / so that the latter explains, as it were, the *Theory*, and the former the *Practice* of the Philosophical Work <note>The "Philosophical Work" refers to the "Great Work" (Magnum Opus) of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> / understanding that each directed the primary focus of his attention toward those ends. 

Furthermore, I live in the confidence / that this so-called *Two-Leaf*, if diligently read / and well understood / will be pleasing and hopefully not useless to many a faithful German lover of the Philosophical Science / which I then wish to every diligent and pious seeker from the bottom of my heart, along with all temporal and eternal well-being! Written in *Hamburg* on the 15th of *June* in the *year* 1673.

->*J. L.*<-

# The Entrance Opened
## To the King’s Closed Palace.
### Preface of the Author.

As I, an anonymous lover of truth and a Philosopher <note>original: "Philosophus"</note>, attained the secrets of Medicine, Chemistry, and Nature in the year after the redemption of the world, one thousand six hundred and forty-five, and in the <margin>33</margin> twenty-third year of my age, I resolved to write this little treatise <note>original: "Tractätlein"</note> so that I might fulfill my duty toward the Sons of Art <term>Sons of Art: A traditional term for initiates and dedicated students of alchemy who are considered spiritual heirs to the "secret" knowledge.</term> and extend a hand to those confused within the labyrinth of error, so that the possessors (Adepts <note>original: "Adepti." Individuals who have successfully created the Philosopher's Stone.</note>) may see that I am their equal and their brother; and so that those who have been led astray by the chatter of deceivers might see and take hold of the light by which they can safely return. Furthermore, I suspect that not a few will be enlightened by this work of mine. These are no mere fables, but actual proofs which I have seen and performed, and which I know—as an Adept will easily conclude and perceive from these lines. Therefore, as I write this for the benefit of my neighbor, let it suffice that I say publicly that no one who has written on this Art has ever written so clearly, and that many times while writing I have laid aside my pen, because I would have preferred to hide the truth under the mask of envy <note>original: "Larve der Mißgunst." In alchemical literature, "envy" refers to the tradition of "Hermetic secrecy," where authors intentionally obscured their recipes to prevent the "unworthy" from gaining power.</note>. But I was compelled to do so by God, who alone knows hearts, to whom alone be honor in eternity! To whom I [do] not

<summary>This preface introduces the author as a young Adept who mastered the secrets of alchemy in 1645. He declares his intention to provide a clear guide for true seekers, despite the traditional alchemical urge to keep such secrets hidden from the world.</summary>
<keywords>Eirenaeus Philalethes, Adept, preface, King's Palace, alchemy, 1645, Sons of Art, secrecy</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s preface from page 9, where he admits he initially felt the urge to hide these secrets out of "envy" (a common alchemical trope where masters withheld information from the unworthy), but ultimately felt compelled to share them. It transitions from the conclusion of the preface into the first chapter of the technical treatise.</meta>

->Chymical Two-Leaf<-

...not been able to resist. Therefore, I conclude without a doubt that many will be blessed with this secret in these final times of the world; for I have written faithfully and have left no doubt for the diligent student in which he is not fully satisfied. I also know that there are many who possess this secret along with me, and I imagine that there are many more whose close friendship I shall soon obtain anew every day. May the holy will of God grant what is pleasing to Him. I recognize myself as unworthy to perform such things; yet I praise the holy will of God, to whom all created things are subject, and for whose sake alone He created, sustains, and protects them.

## Chapter I.

### On the Necessity of the Mercury of the Sages for the Work-Elixir.

Whoever desires to obtain this golden flux <note>original: "guldene Fluß." This refers to the molten state of the alchemical medicine or the "Stone" when it is ready to transform other metals.</note> should know that our gold-making powder, which we call our Stone, is nothing else but gold alone, cooked to the highest degree of purity and subtle stability <term>Fixität: In alchemy, "fixity" refers to the ability of a substance to withstand the heat of fire without evaporating or changing.</term>, to which it can be brought by Nature and ingenious Art. This gold, thus brought to its essence <term>Essenz: The concentrated, spiritualized core of a physical substance.</term>, is called *our gold* and no longer *common gold*, and it is the perfect conclusion of Nature and Art. I could cite all the philosophers <note>original: "Philosophos"</note> in this matter: I need 

<meta>catchword: but</meta>

...but I need no witnesses, for I myself am a possessor, 
and I write more clearly than anyone has done before 
this. Let him believe it who will; let him

he will certainly gain no more reward from it than 
that his excessive ignorance will be seen therein. 
The clever, subtle people may imagine for 
themselves strange and wonderful things, but a 
diligent person will find the truth in the simple 
way of Nature.

Our gold, however, which we desire to have in 
our Work, is of two kinds: namely the ripe, 
fixed <term>Fixität: The quality of a substance that allows it to remain stable and not evaporate when exposed to intense heat.</term>, the yellow Laton <term>Laton: A term used by alchemists to describe a "base" or "unclean" gold-like metal that must be purified before it can be transformed.</term>, whose heart or center-point 
is a pure fire; for this reason, it protects its

the bodily gold is dead before it is—

may thereafter possess a more noble state

<summary>The author asserts his personal mastery of alchemy and defines the "Stone" as being composed of two types of "gold": a male, fixed principle and a female, white/unripe principle. Their union creates the Alchemical Hermaphrodite, where the inner qualities of the metal are "turned outward" to achieve a perfected state.</summary>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the previous discussion regarding the two types of "Philosophical Gold" (the fixed male principle and the unripe female principle) into the central mystery of the work: the "Mercury of the Sages." While the previous page established gold as the starting point, this page identifies Mercury as the essential medium or "wife" required for the transformation.</meta>

# Chemical Two-Leaf

...now it is to be seen that the entire secret consists in Mercury <note>original: "Mercurio"</note>, of which the Philosopher <note>original: "Philosophus"; likely referring to a foundational authority such as Aristotle or Hermes Trismegistus</note> says: In Mercury is everything that the wise seek. Of this, Geber <note>The Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, a celebrated 8th-century chemist whose works were foundational to European alchemy</note> speaks: Praised be the Most High, who has created our Mercury and shared with it such a nature that it overcomes all things. For certainly, if this were not so, the alchemists might boast as much as they wish, yet the alchemical work would be in vain. It is therefore known and manifest that this is not the common mercury, but that of the Sages; for all common mercury is the "man"—that is, corporeal, ordained to a certain kind (*specified* <term>specified: In alchemy, this means a substance that has already reached its final, fixed form (like common metal), making it "dead" because it can no longer be transformed into something else</term>), and dead.

<margin><image-desc>A manicule (pointing hand) in the left margin points toward the following sentence about "Our Mercury".</image-desc></margin>
Our Mercury, however, is spiritual: the living and life-giving "woman." Take note, then, of what kind of Mercury I shall speak. For, as the Philosopher says: Our Mercury is the Salt of the Sages; whoever works without this is like an archer who wants to shoot without a bowstring, and yet it is to be found nowhere upon the earth. It is, however, the "Son" who is prepared by us—not that we created him from nothing, but that we draw him out from those things wherein he is, by means of co-operating Nature, in a wonderful way, through a subtle and ingenious Art.

---

## Chapter II.
### Of the Beginnings from which the Mercury of the Sages is Made.

The intention or the aim of several who work in this Art is this: that they...

<meta>catchword: Mer.</meta>

<summary>This page identifies "Philosophical Mercury" as the vital, spiritual "female" principle of the alchemical work, distinguishing it from common, "dead" quicksilver. ### Purifying **Mercury** in different ways.

For some sublime it with the addition of salts; some clean it of various impurities; others make it living by itself; and thus they believe that when they repeat such works (often), then the **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> of the Sages has been made. This is because our fiery dragon <note>original: "feuriger Drache"; a common alchemical symbol for a potent, corrosive agent or the "secret fire" that dissolves metals</note>, which overcomes everything, is nonetheless permeated by the scent of the Vegetable **Saturnia**; its blood grows together with the sap of **Saturnia** into a wondrous body, and yet it is no body, for it is entirely volatile; and it is also no spirit, for in the fire it appears like a molten metal. ## Chapter VII.

### Concerning the first work of the preparation of the Mercury of the Sages, through the flying eagles.

KNOW, Brother, that the exact preparation of the **Philosophical Eagles** <note>In alchemical symbolism, an "eagle" represents the process of sublimation—where a substance is heated into vapor and then solidified again. Each "flight" of an eagle refers to one such cycle of purification.</note> is held to be the first degree of perfection; to recognize such requires a skillful understanding. For you should not believe that anyone among us came to this science by chance, or through accidental imagination, as the lazy rabble <note>original: "faule Pöbel"; alchemists often distinguished themselves from "puffers" or "charcoal burners" who sought only material wealth without study.</note> without understanding believes; rather, we have exerted ourselves for a great and long time, we have spent many nights without sleep, and endured much labor and toil, so that we might overcome the truth. Therefore, you, diligent student, shall know for certain that you will achieve nothing without toil and labor—understand this to be in the first work; even though in the second work Nature alone completes the task without any laying on of hands <note>Refers to the "Great Work" proper, where the ingredients are sealed in a flask and left to react under heat without further manual interference.</note>, except that an external moderate fire is used for it.

Hear therefore, dear brother, the sayings of the Sages, when they write that one must lead their **eagles** to the **lion** to be devoured; the fewer in number they are, the more violent the battle will be, and the victory will occur more slowly. But the work is best accomplished through the seventh or ninth number <note>The number of times the substance must be distilled or sublimated to reach the required purity.</note>. For the **Mercury of the Sages** is the **Bird of Hermes** <note>The "Bird of Hermes" is a famous symbol for the volatile spirit of Mercury; a common alchemical rhyme states: "The Bird of Hermes is my name, eating my wings to make me tame."</note>; which is sometimes called a goose, sometimes a pheasant, sometimes this or that. ### Chapter VIII.

...easy <note>The word "easy" (German: *leichtes*) completes a sentence from the previous page regarding the final stages of the work.</note>, and of a good root (or beginning), **persistence** and **diligence** are the most noble parts; and truly, the labor of the household servants (which is more to be considered a game and an amusement for the mind) will not accomplish what we so greatly desire according to your wish. Indeed, one must, as **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure and purported author of the *Hermetica*, regarded as the father of alchemy.</note> says, spare no reflection nor labor. Otherwise, what the **Wise Man** <note>A reference to King Solomon; specifically Proverbs 21:25: "The desire of the slothful killeth him; for his hands refuse to labor."</note> foretold in his parables will come true: namely, that the sluggard dies over his desires. And it is no wonder that so many people who occupy themselves with **Alchemy** <note>original: "Alchimie"</note> end up with the **beggar’s staff** <note>A common German idiom meaning to fall into extreme poverty or bankruptcy.</note>. Specifically, they flee from the work and cannot endure the expenses. 

But we, who know these things and have worked them out, have certainly experienced that no work is more vexing than our **preparation**. For this reason, **Morienus** <note>Morienus Romanus, a legendary 7th-century hermit and alchemist who supposedly taught the Umayyad prince Khalid ibn Yazid.</note> earnestly admonishes the King in this regard, saying: That most among the Sages have complained about the tediousness of this work. And I do not wish for this to be understood in a figurative way, as one does not presently consider things as they appear at the beginning of the supernatural work, but rather how we first found them. To make the **matter** <note>The "materia prima" or starting physical substance.</note> fit for the task, the Poet says: **Therein lies the toil, therein lies the labor.** <note>original Latin paraphrase: "Hoc opus, hic labor est," a famous line from Virgil’s *Aeneid* often cited by alchemists to describe the difficulty of the initial stages.</note> And again:

...finally this serpent is pierced, as he has coagulated it through the power of his <term>Sulphur</term> <note>While common sulfur is a yellow mineral, the alchemical "Sulphur" is the active, masculine, and "fiery" principle that gives form and color to metals.</note>. Give it to be understood, therefore, that this Mercury is closer to the first essence of the metals than any other Mercury. For that reason, it enters into the very foundations of the metals and reveals their hidden depths.

### Chymisches Zwey-Blat

...researched [even] the **hyssop** <note>A reference to King Solomon's botanical knowledge described in 1 Kings 4:33: "He described plant life, from the cedar of Lebanon to the hyssop that grows out of walls."</note> that grows out of the wall; who then, being of sound mind, would deny that he should not also have understood the nature of **minerals**, the knowledge of which is no less delightful? But to proceed to the matter at hand, we say that it is to be considered believable that those who initially received this **Magistery** <note>original: "Magisterium"; the mastery of the alchemical process or the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>—among whom **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the founder of alchemy.</note> is to be counted—because they lacked many books, did not at first seek "super-perfection," but only the simple elevation of imperfect things to the **Royal State** <note>The 'Royal State' is a common alchemical term for the perfection of gold.</note>. And when they saw that all **metals** had their origin in **Mercury**, and that **Mercury** was most similar in weight and uniformity to the most perfect metal, **Gold**, they therefore resolved to cook this [Mercury] for the ripening of gold. But they were unable to accomplish this with any fire; they therefore considered that, at the very least, in addition to external heat, an **internal fire** would be necessary to obtain their wish; they then sought this in many things. First, they distilled very **hot waters** <note>In early chemistry, "hot waters" usually refers to strong mineral acids, such as nitric acid (aqua fortis).</note> from the lesser minerals and used them to **corrode** the Mercury. But they could not bring it about through any art in this way that the Mercury changed its internal properties; namely, because all these corrosive waters were only external **active agents** <note>original: "agentia"</note>, according to the measure of the fire, albeit in a different manner. Thus these **solvents** <note>original: "menstrua"; from the Latin 'menstruum,' a term for a solvent used to dissolve solids or extract essences.</note>, as they [called] them...

# Chapter XI.

...that it was completely impossible, because it had no **Metallic Sulfur** <note>original: "Metallischen Schwefel"; the active, "fiery" principle in alchemy that gives metals their specific properties and allows for maturation.</note> within it, even though it was abundantly provided with the purest **Salt of Nature**. Just as they sensed in <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: "Mercurio"; quicksilver</note> a small and only passive sulfur, they found in this **Child of Saturn** <note>original: "Saturnus-Kinde"; a common alchemical name for Antimony, as Saturn rules lead and antimony is its "offspring" or "immature" form.</note> no actual sulfur, but only a potential one. For this reason, it entered into a bond with **Arsenical burning sulfur** and, like a fool, cannot exist without it in a **coagulated form**. It is yet so foolish that it would rather live and "commit adultery" with this enemy, by whom it is held strictly captive, than renounce it and appear in **Mercurial form**.

Therefore, as they pursued an active sulfur, the Wise finally sought and found it hidden very deep in the **House of the Ram** <note>original: "Widders Hause"; a reference to Aries, which in alchemical practice often signifies Iron, or the beginning of the Great Work in the springtime.</note>. This, however, was greedily accepted by the **Child of Saturn**, because it is a pure **Metallic Matter**, very delicate and closely related to the first metallic essence; though robbed of all actual sulfur, it is <gloss>in potential</gloss> <note>original: "in potentiä"</note> capable of receiving sulfur.

Therefore, like a **magnet**, it attracts this to itself, and swallows and hides it in its belly. And the Almighty, so that He might greatly adorn this work, stamps His **Royal Seal** <note>original: "Königliches Siegel"; this refers to the "Star Regulus of Antimony," a star-like crystalline pattern that forms on the surface of the metal when purified specifically with iron.</note> upon it. At this, the Wise rejoiced at once, as they had not only found the sulfur but had seen it prepared. Finally, they resolved to purify **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"</note> by means of this, but they did not succeed, because an **Arsenical, evil poison-**

...was mixed with this **sulfur** swallowed by the **Child of Saturn** <note>original: "Kinde deß Saturni"; referring to Antimony, which in alchemy is often called the "child" of the planet Saturn (Lead) due to their shared properties and proximity in the mineral kingdom.</note>; and although this was now very little in comparison to the quantity it possessed in its mineral nature, it nonetheless prevented all ingress or entrance. 
<margin>A small sketch of a pointing hand indicates this passage.</margin> Therefore, they attempted to temper this evil air <note>The "evil air" likely refers to volatile impurities or arsenical vapors that hinder the metallic union.</note> through the **Doves of Diana** <note>original: "Tauben der Dianæ"; a poetic term for the white, purified sublimate or the cooling, silvery essence used to wash the "blackness" out of the matter.</note>; and they succeeded according to their wish. Then they mixed life with life, and moistened the dry through the moist, and sharpened the passive through the active, and made the dead living through the living. 
Thus, the sky was darkened for a time, which became beautifully bright again after a gentle downpour <note>The darkening refers to the *nigredo* (blackening) stage, followed by a symbolic "rain" or washing that leads to the *albedo* (whitening) stage.</note>. Here, the **Hermaphroditic Mercury** <note>original: "Hermaphroditische Mercurius"; a symbolic figure representing the union of the male (sulfur/active) and female (mercury/passive) principles into a single, potent substance.</note> emerged; they then placed this into the fire and, in not too long a time, it was coagulated; and in its coagulation, they found **Gold** and **Silver** to be entirely pure. 

Finally, they came back to themselves and considered that this purified and not yet coagulated **Mercury** was not yet a metal, but had become volatile and left nothing behind on the bottom during its distillation; therefore, they called it their **unripe gold** and **living silver**. They likewise considered that, if it were the true first essence of gold and still volatile, why should it not also be the **field** in which the sown gold could be multiplied? 
For this reason, they placed the gold into it, and—which easily caused them wonder—the fixed became volatile within it, the hard became soft, and the coagulated was dissolved, so that Nature itself was terrified by it. Therefore, they have

In this way, the living became dead, and the dead became living. The body putrefied <note>Putrefaction (or *nigredo*) was considered a vital stage where the old form of the metal died so a new, perfected form could be born.</note>, and the spirit was heartily resurrected, and the soul was finally raised to a **fifth essence** <note>original: "fünfften Wesen"; also known as the Quintessence, the purest and most concentrated medicinal core of a substance.</note>, namely, to the highest **medicine** <note>original: "Medicin"; in this context, the "Universal Medicine" or the Philosopher's Stone, capable of "healing" base metals into gold or curing human ailments.</note> for animals, metals, and growing things.

# Chapter XII.
## On the Manner and Way of Making the Perfect **Magisterium** <note>original: "Magisterium"; the "Mastery" or the completed Great Work of alchemy.</note>, in General.

We should thank God without ceasing that He has shown us these secrets of nature, which He has hidden from very many eyes. What has therefore been given to us freely by this great Giver, we wish to reveal freely and faithfully to other lovers (of the **Art** <note>The "Art" is the traditional term for alchemy, implying it is both a spiritual and technical craft.</note>). Therefore, you shall know that the greatest secret of our work is nothing other than an often-repeated infusion of natures, one over the other, until the perfectly cooked power is drawn out of the cooked by the raw. To that end, however, first an exact bringing-forth and preparation is required, as well as the joining together of the things which must be taken for this work. Secondly, a good disposition and order of external things. Thirdly, when the matters are thus ordered, a good government <note>In alchemy, "government" or "regimen" refers to the precise control of the furnace's heat over several months.</note> is required.

<sig>C</sig>
<meta>catchword: To</meta>

<vocab>Mastery, Secrets of Nature, Medicine, Metals, Fifth Essence (Quintessence), Infusion, Art, Alchemy</vocab>

<summary>This page describes the final stage of the alchemical process where matter is raised to a "fifth essence" or universal medicine, and begins a new chapter outlining the three pillars of the Great Work: preparation, organization, and governance of the fire.</summary>
<keywords>Quintessence, Magisterium, Universal Medicine, Putrefaction, Alchemical Regimen, Great Work, Secrets of Nature</keywords>

Fourthly, it is necessary that one knows beforehand what will manifest in the work, so that one does not proceed blindly. Fifthly, patience, so that one does not rush the work or govern it thoughtlessly. Of all these things, we wish to speak in an orderly fashion, as one brother (indebted) to another.

# Chapter XIII.
## On the Use of Mature Sulphur in the Work of the Elixir.

We have spoken of the necessity of Mercury and brought forth many things concerning the secret Mercury <note>original: "geheimē Mercurio"; referring to the "Philosophical Mercury," a refined substance believed to be the heart of all metals, rather than common quicksilver.</note>, of which little was known in the world before me, because all chemical books are either full of dark riddles, or deceptive works, or finally full of old, obsolete words. But I have not done so; rather, in this matter, I have submitted my will to Divine Pleasure, whom it pleased in this latter time of the world <note>The author likely refers to the "Last Days," a common 17th-century belief that God was revealing hidden scientific and spiritual secrets before the end of the age.</note> to reveal these treasures to me. Therefore, I no longer fear that (the Art) <note>The "Art" refers to alchemy.</note> will become disrespected—far be it from that; this cannot happen. For true wisdom maintains itself in everlasting honor. O, that gold and silver might once be regarded as lowly as dung! That great idol worshipped by the whole world until now! If that were so, then we who know these secrets would not need to remain so hidden—we who (with mourning and sigh-

He claims his writing is uniquely clear compared to his predecessors and argues that the discovery of the Philosopher's Stone should ideally devalue gold, which the world treats as an idol.</summary>

# Chapter XIII.

...that the death of one principle <note>original: "Anfanges [principii]"; refers to the fundamental source or beginning of a substance.</note> shares the same cycle <note>original: "Umgang [periodum]"; a recurring process or orbital period.</note> with the life of the other. That is, the spirit is coagulated <note>original: "coaguliret"; the process of a liquid thickening or becoming solid.</note> when the body has been dissolved, and thus they both rot together in the form of a slime [or clay] until all parts of the compound <note>original: "zusammen gesetzten [compositi]"</note> are dispersed from one another as a subtle dust. This is then the natural property of our **Mastery** <note>original: "Magisterii"; the specialized knowledge and practice of the alchemical Great Work.</note>: the secret which we have kept so deeply hidden is how to prepare the mercury, which is properly so-called and which, as it is useful for our operation, cannot be found upon the earth; and this for special reasons known only to the **Adepts** <note>original: "Adeptis"; those who have mastered the secrets of alchemy.</note>. In this mercury we amalgamate <note>original: "amalgamiren"; to blend a metal with mercury to form a soft, workable paste.</note> the fine, most highly purified gold—either filed or beaten into thin sheets—very well; we place it in a glass and boil it without ceasing. Now, through the power of our water, the gold is dissolved and returns to its proximate (first) matter <note>original: "Materie"; the raw, primal state of a substance before it takes a specific form.</note>, in which the locked-up life of the gold becomes free and loose, and receives the life of the dissolving mercury, which, in relation to the gold, is exactly what a good soil is to a grain of wheat. In this mercury, the gold is thus dissolved and rots—and it must necessarily be so because nature requires it. # Chemical Double-Leaf

...of a nobler <term>substance</term> <note>original: "Substanz"; the essential matter underlying the alchemical work.</note> which takes onto itself the degrees of power in proportion to the distinction between the properties of the four <term>elements</term> <note>The four fundamental states of matter: earth, air, fire, and water.</note>; that is the nature of our work, that is our entire <term>philosophy</term> <note>original: "Philosophie"; here referring to the theoretical and practical system of alchemy.</note>. We have therefore said that in our work there is nothing secret, except for the <term>mercury</term> <note>original: "Mercurius"; in alchemy, this is not common quicksilver, but a specialized "philosophical" solvent that acts as the life-force of the process.</note> alone, whose **Mastery** <note>original: "Magisterium"; the expert procedure for preparation and control of the alchemical process.</note> is to prepare it in the proper manner, and to unite it with **gold** in the right proportion, and to govern it with such a **fire** as the **mercury** requires. For the **gold** itself does not fear the **fire**; therefore, the governance of the **fire** must be arranged in such a way as the **mercury** can endure, which then requires effort and labor.

But whoever has not prepared his **mercury** in the proper way, even if he adds **gold** to it, his **gold** remains merely common **gold**, as it is united with such a foolish **agent** <note>original: "Agente"; an active chemical force or catalyst. Calling it "foolish" implies it is ineffective or improperly prepared.</note> in which it remains just as unchanged as if it had stayed in the chest; and it will not cast off its bodily nature through any governance of the **fire**. Our **mercury**, however, is not of that nature, and therefore our **gold** is **spermatic** <note>original: "spermatisch"; meaning "seed-like" or containing the potential for life and reproduction.</note>, just like sown wheat-seed is; whereas otherwise that same wheat in the barn remains grain and dead, even if it were buried in a box under the earth (as the people in the **West Indies** <note>original: "West-Indien"; a common 17th-century reference to the Americas and the agricultural practices of indigenous peoples.</note> are accustomed to keep their fruits in pits under the earth, which are well-protected from watery dampness). Accordingly, if a moist vapor from the earth does not come to meet it, it is dead; that is, it remains without fruit, and entirely without any grow-

# Chapter XIII.

wealth. I know there will be many who will criticize this teaching and say: this man asserts that common **gold** and flowing **mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"; here referring to common quicksilver.</note> are the material components of the **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone.</note>; but we know the opposite to be true. Well then, you **philosophers**, search your purses; since you know this, do you also have the **Stone** within them? I, truly, possess it, not through a gift [except from my God alone], nor through theft; I have made it, and I hold it daily in my power. 

Oh, very well! Go to work with your rain-water, May-dew, with your salt; prattle about your seed, which is more powerful than the Devil himself; revile me—do you think that I will be distressed by your scornful talk? I say that **gold** and **mercury** alone are our material components, and I know what I write, just as God knows, who tests all hearts, that I write the truth. 

Thus, you must not accuse me of envy, for I write with an intrepid pen, with a manner of writing unheard of, for the glory of **God**, for the benefit and piety of my neighbor, and out of contempt for the world and wealth. For **Elias Artista** <note>A legendary figure in alchemy, first predicted by Paracelsus, who would supposedly arrive in the "latter days" to reveal the final secrets of nature and alchemy.</note> is already born, and now glorious things are being preached concerning the City of God. I might almost say I possess more wealth than the entire known world is worth, but I may not use the same, because evil rogues craftily lie in wait for me. I am justly indignant and curse this **idolatry** <note>original: "Götzen-Dienst"; literally "idol-service."</note> of **gold** and **silver**, whose value, splendor, and vanity the world prizes so highly. Oh, what a shameful existence! Oh, what a vain nothingness! Do you think that I hide these things out of envy?

### Chapter XIII.

My spirit wells up in my heart for the welfare of all God’s Israel <note>In this context, "Israel" refers to the community of the faithful or the "true believers" in a spiritual and alchemical sense, rather than strictly a geopolitical or ethnic group.</note>. Then virtue alone would be held in honor for the sake of its lovely nature.

## Chapter XIV.

### On the necessary and accidental circumstances
### that generally belong to this work.

We have freed the chemical art from all common errors, and after refuting the deceptions and the presumptuous dreams of those who think themselves wise, we have taught that the Art must be made from gold and mercury <note>original: "Mercurio"</note>. We have shown that the Sun <note>original: "Sol"; in alchemy, Sol is the standard name for both the sun and the metal gold</note> is, without any flowery figures of speech, actually gold; and we have clearly demonstrated without circumlocution that quicksilver is mercury <note>original: "Mercurius"</note>. We have proven that the former is made perfect by nature and is available for purchase, while the latter must be prepared through the Art. We have provided such bright and clear reasons or grounds that unless you wish to be blind in broad daylight, you must understand it. We have publicly confessed that we did not bring this forth from other writings based on the faith we place in them; we have seen it ourselves and know what we faithfully declare. We have made, seen, and possess the great stone, the Elixir. And, in truth, we do not begrudge you this knowledge, but rather wish that you may learn it from these writings. We have

<summary>The author clarifies that the "Great Work" of alchemy is based on real gold and mercury rather than symbolic or deceptive ingredients, claiming personal success in creating the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

<header>Chapter XV.</header>

I have furthermore made it known that the preparation of the 
true Philosophical Mercury <term>Philosophical Mercury: the "soul" or essential quicksilver of the philosophers, distinct from common mercury</term> is difficult—indeed, so difficult 
that the special grace of God is necessary for it, should anyone 
desire to come to a perfect knowledge of the same. I have—
which no one before me has done—brought forth such things 
concerning its preparation that I can do no more, 
unless I were to give a recipe outright; which I 
have also done, only I did not call the things by their 
own names. Now it remains for me to describe 
the use and the procedure, through which you can easily 
perceive whether the Mercury is good or deficient; 
and when you understand that, you can change 
and improve it according to your desire. Now, when you 
have the animated Mercury <term>animated Mercury: mercury that has been "quickened" or purified to a state where it can act upon gold</term> and the gold, there 
remains the accidental cleaning <note>In alchemy, "accidental" refers to external impurities rather than the essential nature of the substance</note> as well of the 
Mercury as of the gold; thereafter their marriage, 
and thirdly, the governance <note>original: "Regierung"; the careful management of fire and heat during the process</note>.

---

## Chapter XV.

### On the accidental cleaning of Mercury and Gold.

Gold is found perfect in the earth; therefore it 
is sometimes found in small pieces and sand. 
If you can have it in this pure state, then it is clean 
enough. If not, then clean it through antimony <note>original: "Antimonium"; used to purge impurities from gold</note>, 
or upon the cupel <note>original: "Test"; a porous ceramic tray used in refining</note>, or separate it with aqua fortis <note>original: "aqua fort"; nitric acid</note>, 
provided that you first granulate the gold. 
Afterward, let it flow in a smelting fire and file it; 
then it is ready.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the purification of materials. Having previously mentioned the cleaning of gold, the author now focuses on the "essential" and "accidental" purification of Mercury using a series of distillations and chemical washes.</meta>

<page-num>50</page-num>
<header>*Chymical Two-Sheet*</header>

Mercury, however, requires an internal and essential purification, which consists in the gradual addition of the true Sulfur <note>In alchemy, Sulfur represents the active, masculine principle that "fixes" the volatile Mercury</note>, according to the number of Eagles <term>Eagles: an alchemical term for the number of times Mercury is sublimated or distilled; each "flight" of the eagle represents one purification cycle</term>; then it is purified from its very foundation. Beyond this, however, it also requires an accidental cleaning <note>As noted on the previous page, "accidental" refers to the removal of external or surface impurities (dross) rather than changing the inner nature of the metal</note> to wipe away the external filth that it casts out from its center. This work is not strictly necessary, yet it accelerates the Great Work, and therefore it is not unhelpful. Therefore, take your Mercury, which you have prepared through the appropriate number of Eagles, and sublimate it three times with common salt and iron dross; grind it at the same time with vinegar and a little Sal ammoniac <term>Sal ammoniac: ammonium chloride, a salt used to clean metals and aid in sublimation</term> until the Mercury is no longer visible. Then dry it and distill it through a glass retort <term>Retort: a glass vessel with a long, downward-curving neck used for distilling substances via heat</term>. Increase the fire gradually until all the Mercury has risen. Repeat this four times. Afterward, boil the Mercury in distilled vinegar for one hour in a flask or glass with a wide bottom and a narrow neck; shake it well from time to time, then pour off the vinegar and wash away the acidity with spring water, repeatedly poured over it. Then dry the Mercury, and you will marvel at its luster. You could also wash it with urine or vinegar and salt and be spared the sublimation, then only distilling it four times after you have completed all the Eagles, without any additions; however, you must wash out the iron retort each time with ash and water. Finally, boil it in distilled vinegar for half a day, stir it well occasionally, and pour out the black-colored vinegar,

<margin>53</margin>Many people strive to obtain the **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"</note> of gold; others, to obtain the **Mercury** of silver; but in vain. But if the secret of our Gold and <term>Mercury: In alchemy, this is not common quicksilver, but a "philosophical" solvent or life-force used to break down and "kill" metals so they can be reborn.</term> is unknown to you until now, then withdraw your hand from the work, for you have nothing but loss to expect.

Put them together as mentioned above, and place them in the fire; provide enough warmth that it boils and "sweats," and that its <note>original: "Schweiß ſich circulire." This describes the process of reflux, where vapor rises, condenses, and flows back down into the base of the glass vessel.</note> sweat circulates. Do this day and night for ninety days and nights; then you will see that this Mercury has separated all the elements of the common gold from one another and brought them back together again.

Cook for another fifty days following this, and you will see that the common gold has been transformed by our Mercury into *our* Gold, which is a <term>medicine of the first order: An early stage of alchemical achievement; it possesses healing properties but is not yet potent enough for the final transmutation of metals.</term> This <term>Sulfur: The active, combustible principle in alchemy, representing the "soul" or internal fire of a substance.</term> is now our Sulfur, but it will not yet <term>tinge: The alchemical ability of the Stone to "stain" or transform the entire mass of a base metal into gold.</term>. And believe me, many <term>Philosophers: A term used here for alchemists—those who studied the "natural philosophy" of God’s creation.</term> have worked in this way and attained the truth; however, it is a very tedious path and is suited only for great lords.

For you must not imagine that once you have obtained this sulfur, you immediately possess the Stone and its true matter—which you might otherwise seek and find in an imperfect thing within a single week. This is our easy and special path, which God has [given/revealed] to the poor...

If you wish to work with common gold, then see to it that you bring about the **Marriage of Venus** <term>Marriage of Venus: (German: *Venus-Heyrath*) In alchemy, this refers to the union of metallic principles, often involving copper (associated with Venus) or the receptive, "feminine" aspect of the matter.</term> with great care; afterward, lay it in its bed. Therefore, if you know the art, extract our gold from our **Mercury**; thus you will accomplish the work from a single thing which—believe me—is more perfect than all perfection in the world, as the **Philosopher** <note>A general title for an alchemical authority or sage.</note> speaks when he says: "If you know how to make the work from Mercury alone, you will be a discoverer of the most precious work." In this work there are no superfluities; rather, by the living God, everything is brought entirely to purity because the **action** <note>original: "actio"</note> occurs in one thing. However, if you begin the process with common gold, the acting and the suffering <note>In alchemy, "suffering" refers to the passive state of the matter being transformed or "acted upon" by a solvent or heat.</note> occur in two things. The middle substance of both is taken alone, and the impure is cast away. ## Chapter XXIII.

### Concerning the various governances of this work.

Live in the certain confidence, diligent student, that nothing in the entire work is withheld from you except the <term>governance</term> <note>original: *Regierung*; the specific method of managing the fire's temperature and duration.</note>. Concerning this, it is true what the <term>Philosopher</term> <note>original: *Philosophus*; likely referring to Hermes Trismegistus or Aristotle, often cited as authorities in alchemical texts.</note> says: whoever knows this properly (and in detail), the princes and great lords of this world will honor him. And I swear to you by my faith and honor, that if this alone were openly revealed to the light of day, even fools would laugh at the Art. For once one knows this, all the rest is nothing other than "women’s work and children’s play" <note>original: *Werck der Weiber und Spiel der Kinder*; a common alchemical phrase suggesting that once the heat is correctly set, the process proceeds easily by itself, like boiling a pot.</note>—that is, merely a cooking. Therefore, the wise have hidden this secret in the highest and most artful manner, and believe firmly that we have basically done the same. However, because I have promised to treat everything sincerely in this little treatise, it shall be my duty to add something...

...to do / so that I do not deceive the understanding readers in their hope and work. # Chapter XXIV.

## Concerning the first governance / which is that of Mercury.

And first we wish to speak of the governance of <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurii*; representing the initial, volatile stage of the process where the "spirit" of the matter is managed.</note> / a secret which has never been revealed by the Sages. ## Chapter XXVII.

## Chapter XXXIII.
### On the Multiplication of the Stone.

No other labor belongs to this process / than that one should take the perfected Stone / and set one part of it to three or at most four parts of the **Mercury** of the first order <note>Mercury of the first order refers to the initial, highly purified "Philosophical Mercury" or solvent prepared at the start of the Great Work.</note> and govern it with appropriate fire for seven days long / in a very well-closed vessel / thus all the **regimens** <term>Regimens (Regimente): The successive stages of the alchemical process, traditionally marked by specific color changes (black, white, yellow, red) and governed by different "planetary" heats.</term> will reappear with the greatest delight / and you will find everything multiplied with thousand-fold power / according to the quality of the Stone before its multiplication. And if you will try this once more / you will go through all the regimens in three days / and the **Medicine** <term>Medicine (Medicin): A common term for the Philosopher's Stone, emphasizing its ability to "cure" base metals of their impurities and restore health to the human body.</term> will multiply yet again with thousand-fold power; and if you desire to repeat it once more / you will carry the **Work** through all regimens and colors in one natural day <note>A "natural day" typically refers to a full 24-hour cycle.</note> / and that will even happen in one hour / if you try it yet again / and finally you will never be able to fully fathom the **virtue** <note>original: "Tugend"; here meaning the inherent power, potency, or miraculous efficacy.</note> of your Stone. It will be so great / that you will not be able to grasp it with your intellect / provided you persist steadfastly in this work.

->Forget not now to thank God unceasingly /<-
->for you have now the entire treasure<-
->of Nature in your pos-<-
->session.<-

## Chapter XXXIV.

### How one should perform Projection.

Take your perfected Stone / as has been mentioned / either the white or the red / and take, according to the nature of the **Medicine**, four parts of Gold or Silver / let it melt in a clean crucible / then add to it as much of your red or white Stone / according to the type of the melted metal / and when it is well mixed / pour it out into a casting mold <note>original: "Gießpuckel"; a conical iron mold used by alchemists and assayers to cool molten metal.</note> / so it will be a mass / that allows itself to be made into powder; Of this mixture take one part and ten parts of well-purified **Mercury** <term>Mercurii (Mercury): While often a philosophical symbol, here it refers to physical quicksilver used as a medium for the transformation.</term> / heat the **Mercury** / until it begins to crackle / then carry your mixture into it / which will permeate it in an instant / let it flow with increased fire / so it will entirely become a **Medicine** of the lower order <note>A "Medicine of the lower order" refers to a diluted or prepared form of the Stone that is more suitable for large-scale transmutation of base metals.</term>; Then take one part of this **Medicine** / and cast it in flux over any purified metal / namely, as much as your Stone is able to tint <note>original: "tingiren"; the alchemical ability to "color" or transform the internal nature of a metal.</note> / so you will have such fine Gold and Silver / the likes of which nature itself will not give any purer. However / it is better / that one adds (the Medicine) gradually / until the **Tincture** <term>Tinctur (Tincture): The active transmuting essence or "dye" that changes the base metal into a noble one.</term> ceases / for in this way it will spread further / because if only a little is cast upon so much / where the **projection** <term>Projection: The final stage of the Great Work, involving the "throwing" of the Stone onto molten base metals to achieve transmutation.</term> does not happen upon the **Mercury** / one will suffer significant damage to the **Medicine**

# Chapter XXXV.
## On the manifold uses of this art.

Whoever has once perfectly completed this art through the blessing of GOD / I do not know / what more he could wish for himself in this whole world / except / that he might be safe from all evil deceit and cunning / and might serve his GOD without ceasing. But it would be a vanity / indeed the highest vanity of all / to strive for the favor of the common man with ostentation / indeed, those who know this art / do not even ask for such things / but rather they annihilate and despise them. Whoever, therefore, is blessed by God with this talent / has this following field of delights before him.

Firstly / if he were to live a thousand years / and had to maintain a thousand times a thousand people daily / he would yet have no lack / because he can increase the **Stone** as he wishes both in weight / as well as in power / so that he could **tint** <term>tint: From the Latin "tingere," meaning to color or imbue; in alchemy, this refers to the Stone's power to permeate and transform the very nature of a base metal into a precious one.</term> all imperfect metals / that are to be found in the world / whenever he wished / into true gold or silver.

# A Very Excellent Chemical <note>In this period, "chemical" (*Chimischer*) was synonymous with alchemical.</note> Treatise by Brother Ferrarius, Written to the Pope.

TO the supreme dignity of Your Holiness, for a true explanation of the thing which you seek, and for your comfort and delight, I, Brother Ferrarius, Your Holiness’s most humble servant, have written this short little work, into which I have placed nothing else than an extract from the sayings of the Sages. I have primarily followed the opinion of the Philosopher Geber <note>Geber refers to the famous alchemist Jabir ibn Hayyan, or the Latin "Pseudo-Geber," whose works were foundational to European alchemy.</note>, who is the most eminent in this precious art, so that one might avoid fruitless and wasted expenses, as well as the useless labors of the people. 

And so that nothing may be left in doubt, several obstacles shall be cited at the beginning, by which the artist is hindered in the work so that he may not attain the true end, while also citing what the artist of this art must himself possess. Secondly, the reasons of those who deceitfully destroy this art because of its uncertainty and inability, and say that it is not true; whereby it shall also be demonstrated that their reasons are false and without truth. Thirdly, the natural beginnings of this most noble thing shall be treated, and the way and manner how such must be put together one after another—

<summary>This page serves as the formal preface to Brother Ferrarius's alchemical treatise addressed to the Pope, establishing the author's intent to summarize the wisdom of Geber and defend the legitimacy of alchemy.</summary>
<keywords>Brother Ferrarius, Pope, Geber, alchemy, chemical treatise, alchemical theory, philosophical principles</keywords>

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>This page continues the list of topics from the preface of Brother Ferrarius’s treatise and begins the discussion of the first requirement for an alchemist: a sharp and natural understanding of nature’s laws.</meta>

# Brother Ferrarius

...be handled. Fourthly, we shall deal with its effect. Fifthly, the artificial beginnings <note>In alchemy, "artificial beginnings" usually refers to the laboratory processes initiated by the artist, as opposed to "natural beginnings" occurring in the earth.</note> of this thing follow, by means of which this most excellent work is accomplished. Sixthly, the **instruments** and **vessels** in which this thing is worked out will be indicated.

Regarding the first point, I say that anyone who does not possess a natural keenness of mind and understanding—one who knows how to subtly and ingeniously explore the natural beginnings and the foundation of nature within nature, the processes of generation and mixing, as well as the arts which nature can attain and comprehend through the properties of its operation—that person will not find the true root of this most precious science. This is evident in those who are quite stubborn, and whose understanding is empty of all reflection, so that they hardly understand common matters and can only with difficulty grasp and comprehend things that are standard practice.

Thus we also see that many easily imagine something for themselves, believing that every fantasy by which they persuade themselves to have found the truth is sufficient for this purpose; and yet it is all fantastic and not at all in accordance with reason—indeed, it is full of error and does not agree at all with natural beginnings. For their head, which is full of vapors <note>A common early modern metaphor suggesting that "fumes" from the body or laboratory cloud the mind, preventing clear logical thought.</note>, cannot understand or grasp the true <term>intention</term> <note>original: *Intention*; here meaning the inherent purpose, direction, or "plan" hidden within natural substances.</note> of natural things. We also find many who have an unstable mind; they fall from one opinion to another, wanting now this, then that, just like those who soon believe this, then another—

### Chemical Treatise

...want something else without any foundation of rational cause; but shortly thereafter they desire little more and believe something else, and likewise want something else again. These people are so inconsistent that they cannot or may not accomplish even the slightest thing they aim for, but rather leave it unfinished. There are also others who despise this science <term>science</term> <note>original: *Wissenschafft*; in this context, it refers to alchemy as a structured body of knowledge or "scientia," rather than modern laboratory science.</note> and do not believe at all that it exists. They cite the reasons of Geber <note>Geber is the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, the most famous figure in medieval alchemy; his texts were considered the ultimate authority.</note> and others, according to which this science cannot exist, nor contain any truth. Therefore, it remains for me to perform the other task I promised: to refute those reasons and to prove openly that they are false.

And first, I will show what qualities an **artist** <note>original: *Künstler*; the standard term for a practicing alchemist, who "perfects" nature through the "art" of chemistry.</note> of this art must possess. For this, I take a chapter from Geber as an example, who teaches all people to observe certain caution and signs so that they do not fall into danger or offense. I shall also add what I myself have seen and experienced in such matters.

Holy Father <note>A formal address, likely to a high-ranking religious figure or the author's patron, reflecting the clerical status of "Brother" Ferrarius.</note>, an artist of this work must be learned and perfect in the sciences of natural wisdom. For one may have naturally as profound an understanding of this mastery as he wishes, yet he will not achieve its final goal if he has not also obtained wisdom through study, following the way of nature. For as the wise say: what one does not attain through natural understanding, such a deficiency he must compensate for through study. The artist must also, with the deepest...

For no matter how much great knowledge <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: *Wissenschafft*; in this context, it refers to the structured study of alchemy.</note> he has acquired through formal instruction, if he is not also aided by **natural perception**, he will not be invited to such a precious banquet—that is, he will never attain the practical benefit of this most noble art. For, in due time, he will correct his errors through his own diligence, though he would find no means to do so if he relied upon book-learning alone. Likewise, by means of the knowledge gained through study, he will be able to remedy his errors in time—errors he could not avoid through natural perception alone. For, as the Sages say, the art is helped by the intellect, while the intellect is diligently guided by the art.

Likewise, it is also necessary that the **artist** be of a constant will during the work, so that he does not suddenly undertake to try now this, now that; for this art does not consist of a multitude of things. For according to the pronouncement of the Sages, it is **one stone**, **one medicine**, in which the entire mastery <term>mastery</term> <note>original: *Meisterschafft*; the successful completion of the alchemical process, often called the Great Work.</note> consists. To this, no foreign thing is added, nor is anything taken from it, except for that which is superfluous and must be separated during the preparation. It is also good that an artist of this art knows well the beginnings and the original roots belonging to the essence of the work, considering that—as the Sages say—he who does not know the beginning will not find the end. ## Chemical Treatise.

**Treasure and gift of God** / **Holy Father** / I pray with bent knees and with all humility and reverence / that you preserve and seal the same most diligently and secretly in the shrine of your heart / and reveal it to no man, even if it pleases you. Since the Philosophers <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Philosophi*; here referring to the ancient alchemical authorities and masters of the "Stone."</note> in their books wish countless misfortunes upon those who reveal this science to the unworthy. Therefore, it shall be revealed to no one / except to him whom one knows to have a faithful heart. For all the Sages have marvelously hidden this science under flowery <note>original: *verblümten*; meaning metaphorical, allegorical, or coded language intended to obscure the meaning from the uninitiated.</note>, doubtful words and riddles / and did so for this reason / so that the world would not be devastated by it.

Likewise, it is also good / that he be moderate and slow to anger / and does not suddenly, out of violent fury, annihilate and destroy the work now begun. # Of Brother Ferrari

...remain stuck in doubt / or since he, having perhaps already come near to the end of this mastery <term>mastery</term> <note>original: *Meisterschafft*; the complete knowledge and successful execution of the alchemical process.</note> through his reflection, has consumed his expenses / and he thus / like a wretched soul / could not attain the true final result <term>final result</term> <note>original: *Endschafft*; the successful conclusion or the production of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>: Just like those / who from the beginning do not know this / wastefully bring their entire fortune to nothing / but when they are no longer far from it / they have nothing left / with which they can work / wherefore they must finally succumb to a twofold sorrow / both / because they have spent their money on useless things / and also / because they lose the most noble science / which they have quite zealously sought. [...to] give thanks, and share with the poor, and know that all the Sages cry out and say: It is a gift of God, who gives it to whom He wills, and denies it to whom He wills. Or if he does not possess the science <note>In this context, *Wissenschafft* refers to the practical and theoretical knowledge of alchemy.</note> at all, and yet wishes to work, he should always remember God, for He Himself is Wisdom, Mercy, Truth, and Life, always just.

He should also reveal this secret <term>secret</term> <note>original: *Geheimnüß*; the specific processes and materials used to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> to no one, for if he tells it to one person or another, he will perhaps be despised, and people will call him a deceiver and an evil person, and he will gain a bad reputation, just as once happened to one who was arrested and detained for a month and more.

Likewise, a famous physician <term>physician</term> <note>original: *Medicus*.</note> in Austria made the green stone <note>original: *herbalem lapidem*; literally "herbal stone." In alchemical literature, the "green" stage or "green lion" often refers to a potent mineral acid or a specific state of the matter before it reaches perfection.</note> and offered it to a King in Hungary. This stone weighed half a mark <term>mark</term> <note>A traditional unit of weight for precious metals, approximately 230 grams or 8 ounces.</note>, wherefore he said to the King: Lord King, good news! You should know that I have made the Philosopher's Stone <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>original: *Stein der Weisen*.</note>, which is nothing other than drinkable gold <term>drinkable gold</term> <note>original: *trinckliches Gold* or *Aurum Potabile*; a legendary medicinal elixir made from gold, believed to be a universal cure-all.</note>, and a person can be cleansed and cured of his leprosy <term>leprosy</term> <note>original: *Aussatz*; a term used historically for various chronic skin conditions and infectious diseases.</note> with it. The King asked whether this was an alchemical work <note>original: *Alchimistisches Werck*.</note>; when the physician said, "Yes, Lord," the King immediately cast suspicion upon him, so that—

# Brother Ferrari

->### Brother Ferrari’s Treatise<-

...[because] he was a physician, in the belief that he was a deceiver; and [the King] thereupon commanded the physicians that they should examine him. These **physicians** <term>physicians</term> <note>original: *Medici*.</note> then said to him: "Master, if your work were alchemical, it would not be useful to the King, for we know from your rules that gold should have a sweet taste <note>In alchemical theory, the "Gold of the Philosophers" or the perfected Stone was often described as having a pleasant, sweet, or honey-like quality, distinct from the bitter or metallic taste of common minerals.</note>, and that which you have brought has a completely bitter taste; and thus we do not understand what the **Philosophers** <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Philosophi*; referring to the ancient and medieval alchemical authorities.</note> meant by the 'sweet taste'."

And so the aforementioned envious and ignorant physicians, who did not understand the sayings of the Sages in this regard, and lacked knowledge of the things mentioned before, brought the said Master to his death.

Therefore, if it should indeed happen that someone must go to a prince—which, for the reasons cited, is by no means advisable—if it does occur, he should bring him no more of the said **medicine** <term>medicine</term> <note>original: *Medicin*; the Philosopher's Stone was frequently called the "Universal Medicine" because it was believed to cure both "sick" (base) metals and human illnesses.</note> at one time than he can take without harm: namely, the weight of a lentil, with wine or milk; and a careful physician should look well to see how heavy the medicine must be.

Regarding this, our teacher also adds that one should not call our work an alchemical work, but a **philosophical work**, and it is indeed truly called so, so that the minds of men are not led astray; because the envious and ignorant, with such a manner of speaking, imply a deceiver, and they wish to see him no further, but only laugh at him and take him for a **dreamer** <term>dreamer</term> <note>original: *Phantasten*; in the 17th century, this referred to someone who pursued delusions or "fantasies" instead of true science.</note>.

<summary>This page warns of the dangers of presenting alchemical discoveries to royalty, recounting how a master was executed due to the envy and literal-mindedness of court physicians. It advises using the term "philosophical work" instead of "alchemy" to avoid the stigma of being labeled a fraud or dreamer.</summary>

The **Wise** <note>Original: *Weisen*. Often refers to the "Sages" or established masters of alchemy.</note> say that the ignorant artisans, who desire to follow Nature in everything, [are mistaken]. To thicken or densify a substance through heat or evaporation, a crucial stage in creating the "Philosopher's Stone."</note> the metals and brings them into a single essence—because all of this is impossible for them and entirely unknown, for the reason that Nature—as we have said—performs this in the hidden **veins of the earth** <term>veins of the earth</term> <note>Original: *Erd-Gängen*. The subterranean fissures or passages where alchemists believed metals "grew" like plants.</note>.

Therefore, when it is demonstrated in the first proofs that they do not know the philosophical proportion of the elements, the way they are mixed together, how the uniform heat thickens and composes the natural metals, and other causes and accidental things that follow Nature's operation—I certainly admit it. But nevertheless, they do not for that reason abandon this **blessed science** <note>Original: *gebenedeyte Wissenschafft*. Alchemists viewed their work as a sacred, God-given pursuit rather than a mere secular craft.</note>.

The reason is that the aforementioned natural beginnings do not enter into their work, nor can they be adapted to it; instead, they take other natural beginnings and another natural way of generating their metals, in which they can follow and imitate Nature. Should one say that the Wise indeed had a desire for such science, but they did not find it: to that I give my answer and say that it is false. For as I read in the **Books of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophorum Büchern*</note>, some among the most distinguished...

->Wise<-

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the "Philosophical Medicine" (the Philosopher's Stone), transitioning from the theoretical definitions of its matter to a personal address to the "Holy Father" (the patron) regarding the difficulty and secrecy of the alchemical "Artifice."</meta>

# Brother Ferrari #

,, ...nature, which the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*</note> call **Quicksilver** <note>Mercury</note>; and to an earthly, very subtle nature, which the Philosophers call **Sulfur**, through an **Artifice** <term>Kunſt-Stück</term> <note>A specialized alchemical procedure or "masterpiece" of secret knowledge.</note> which the wise have wished to hide in a wonderful way, and have nowhere taught in its entirety, but rather scattered and divided it into pieces throughout their books. Regarding this Artifice, **Holy Father**, I do not venture to speak, for it is impossible for you to attempt it, nor would it be advisable for you to let another attempt it on your behalf. It would also take too long to recount everything necessary for the aforementioned Artifice. But if it pleases Your Holiness, I will explain it in person: for I do not intend to hide anything from you, but rather to teach everything necessary for this Work, briefly and faithfully.
,, Yet the aforementioned Artifice is tedious, costly, and very laborious, and very dangerous to the inexperienced, as I have experienced in my own person.

It should be noted, however, that the Philosophers have called this Quicksilver and this Sulfur "one thing," and it is truly so; for it is one thing and has come out of one thing, as I explained to you at another time.

That it is one thing is proven because they sometimes call the mentioned Quicksilver "Sulfur" and the Sulfur "Quicksilver," and the whole together "Quicksilver and Sulfur." All Philosophers state in many places that it is a single thing alone. For **Lucas** <note>Likely referring to Lucas the Prophet, a figure frequently cited in the *Turba Philosophorum*, one of the oldest and most influential Latin alchemical texts.</note> says: You shall—

# Brother Ferrari #

Now it remains to treat of the **efficient cause** <term>efficient cause</term> <note>In the philosophy of the time, the "efficient cause" is the primary agent or force that initiates a change or produces an effect.</note>. The active agent, however, that moves the matter toward **corruption** <note>original: *corruption*; the necessary process of breaking down or decaying a substance so that it can be reborn in a new form.</note> is heat, which is the moving instrument toward corruption and putrefaction; indeed, there is no other active thing in the world. And this is what the **Philosopher Alphidius** <note>An authority often cited in medieval alchemy, likely referring to an Arabic source translated into Latin and German.</note> says:

> Children, you should know that the active being in this world is a single one, namely heat. But when heat is gone, there is no motion; the root of motion and the effect of the **disposition** <note>original: *disposition*; the specific arrangement or state of the material.</note> in the matter is heat.

But because there are many degrees of heat or of fire, one must see what that same fire is, and in what degree it exists. Truly, this fire is the heat of **horse manure** <note>original: *Pferde-Mistes*; alchemists used the natural, steady heat produced by decomposing manure to maintain a constant temperature for weeks or months.</note>. Therefore, **Alphidius** says in the 4th chapter of his book:

> It is cooked with fire, which I will show you, and that is: let it be hidden in the moist **horse dung** <note>original: *Roß-Mist*.</note>, which is the "Fire of the Wise," namely a moist and dry fire; and that is warm in the first degree, and moist in the second.

Therefore **Vincentius** <note>Likely Vincent of Beauvais, a 13th-century Dominican friar and encyclopedist.</note> says:

> Fire is of many kinds, and its heat of many sorts. Some fire is warm in the first degree, and moist in the second, namely the fire of the **horse’s belly** <note>original: *Pferde-Bauchs*; a metaphor for the gentle, internal heat of the manure-bath (the *balneum ventris equini*), which mimics the warmth of a womb or stomach.</note>, the property of which is not to destroy the oil—that is, the matter—but to increase it because of its moisture; for other fires destroy the dryness. For this heat alone is even and tempered; and such heat is most necessary in the **birth** <note>original: *Gebehrung*; the generation or creation of the alchemical stone.</note> of this thing, because **Geber** <note>The Latin name for Jabir ibn Hayyan, the "Father of Chemistry."</note> says that they must be entirely subtle bellies...

For nothing enters, and nothing is **fixed**, immersed, composed, **thickened**, changed, embraced, or joined together that is not also mixed. It is therefore necessary to see what this "mixing" is; for once it is understood through its description, all the other terms will also be understood.
